```text
1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related}
2	
3	\paragraph{Self-consistency and test-time scaling.}
4	Self-consistency decoding \cite{wang2023selfconsistency} improves reasoning by majority voting over chain-of-thought samples, assuming a discrete answer space.
5	Extensions to free-form text use LLM-based comparison \cite{chen2024usc}, atomic sub-claim decomposition \cite{thirukovalluru2024atomic}, or cross-sample hallucination detection \cite{manakul2023selfcheckgpt}.
6	For test-time scaling \cite{zhang2025tts}, \citet{snell2024scaling} characterize compute scaling laws and \citet{brown2024monkeys} show best-of-$N$ gains across tasks.
7	Recent reward-free selectors include confidence-weighted voting \cite{taubenfeld2025cisc}, KL-based self-certainty \cite{kang2025selfcertainty}, adaptive sampling \cite{kim2026reasc}, ranked aggregation \cite{wang2025rankedvoting}, and latent-space consistency \cite{oh2025latentsc}.
8	Concurrently, \citet{feng2025optimal} and \citet{wang2025make} adapt self-consistency to difficulty-aware settings, echoing our finding that signal rankings shift with task complexity; \citet{li2025llms} propose generative self-aggregation for IE but focus on output fusion rather than confidence-based selection.
9	All of the above target reasoning/QA with discrete answers or specific aggregation strategies.
10	Our work extends self-consistency to set-valued structured outputs and identifies the correlation-selection gap: strong quality estimation ($\rho{=}0.38$) coexists with selection failure ($p{>}0.39$).
11	
12	\paragraph{Consistency for structured IE.}
13	\citet{nedellec2026consistency} study consistency--accuracy correlations for LLM-based NER/RE, corroborating our quality-estimation findings.
14	Their work leaves open three questions we address: (i)~whether structural matching improves on surface-level agreement (which they acknowledge ``overlooks semantically consistent outputs''); (ii)~whether quality estimation translates to best-of-$N$ selection; and (iii)~whether findings generalize across a difficulty gradient.
15	Our Soft Jaccard signal addresses (i) through type-aware bipartite matching with token-level span overlap; our selection analysis (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}) directly addresses (ii); and our three-dataset evaluation spanning easy (CoNLL) to hard (SciERC) tasks addresses (iii).
16	CoCoA \cite{vashurin2025cocoa} fuses model confidence with inter-sample consistency for QA/summarization but does not address structured extraction.
17	Our evaluation builds on generative IE work \cite{zhou2024uniner, sainz2024gollie, josifoski2022genie, li2023codeie, wadhwa2023revisiting, wang2023instructuie} and constrained decoding \cite{willard2023guided}.
18	
19	\paragraph{Uncertainty estimation and calibration.}
20	Surveys cover confidence estimation \cite{geng2024confidence}, selective abstention \cite{wen2025know}, uncertainty quantification \cite{xia2025survey}, and calibration \cite{hobelsberger2025systematic} for LLMs; \citet{xiong2024llm} find LLMs tend toward overconfidence, and \citet{heo2025uncertainty} show calibration degrades with task complexity.
21	Semantic uncertainty \cite{kuhn2023semantic} clusters generations by meaning; \citet{kossen2024semantic} distill this into probes; P(True) \cite{kadavath2022language} queries model confidence directly; \citet{malinin2021uncertainty} distinguish data and model uncertainty in autoregressive models.
22	Our structural signals instead measure agreement over output \textit{structure}.
23	Neural networks are often miscalibrated \cite{guo2017calibration, desai2020calibration}; deep ensembles \cite{lakshminarayanan2017ensembles} and MC-dropout \cite{gal2016dropout} obtain uncertainty from model variation, while our approach requires no model modification.
24	Prior NER uncertainty work spans Bayesian inference for sequence labeling \cite{xiao2019uncertainty}, active learning under uncertainty \cite{shelmanov2021active}, evidential deep learning \cite{zhang2023ener}, verification-augmented NER \cite{kim2024verifiner}, and uncertainty transmission across label sequences \cite{he2024uncertainty}---all within discriminative paradigms.
25	Conformal prediction \cite{fisch2021conformal, angelopoulos2021conformal} provides coverage guarantees; trained verifiers \cite{cobbe2021verifiers, lightman2024verify, venktesh2025trust} and reward models \cite{wang2024mathshepherd} learn per-sample scoring.
26	Our selection strategies instantiate MBR decoding \cite{eikema2022sampling} with structural utility functions; despite MBR's success in translation \cite{freitag2022high} and other generation tasks \cite{jinnai2024mbr}, we find utility-quality misalignment at the per-sample level for structured IE (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
27	Selective prediction \cite{geifman2017selective} and confidence-based IE filtering \cite{culotta2004confidence} establish the broader framework.
28	
```

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Test-time scaling through repeated sampling promises quality gains for LLM-based information extraction, but whether confidence signals can identify the best output among multiple candidates remains untested for structured tasks.
3	Under constrained decoding---the standard regime for schema-compliant IE---we evaluate five confidence signals from three families across NER and relation extraction on three benchmarks with two 8B-parameter model families.
4	These signals achieve strong quality estimation, yet none significantly improves sample selection over greedy decoding despite 13--18 percentage points of oracle headroom.
5	Four of five signals are instance-level by construction and cannot rank candidates; the key empirical question is whether log-probability---the sole per-sample signal---or consensus-proximity selection can bridge this gap.
6	Neither succeeds: log-probability scores are compressed into effective ties for 60\% of instances under constrained decoding, while consensus-proximity (MBR decoding) fails because utility correlates with quality across instances but not within them.
7	Where log-probability compression is absent, selection recovers significant gains ($+2.9$~pp, $p{=}0.01$), identifying constrained-decoding score compression as the primary bottleneck.
8	Signal rankings further depend on task complexity, with findings consistent across model families.
9	\end{abstract}
10	
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Within the constrained decoding regime standard for structured IE, we demonstrate that consistency signals are effective quality estimators ($\rho$ up to 0.38, AUROC up to 0.75) but fail at sample selection---the \textit{correlation-selection gap}.
4	By construction, four of five signals assign identical scores to all candidates from the same input; log-probability, the only per-sample signal, also fails ($p{>}0.39$) despite 13--18~pp oracle headroom due to constrained-decoding-induced score compression.
5	Where compression is mitigated, LP becomes a significant selector ($+2.9$~pp, $p{=}0.01$); consensus-proximity selection (MBR decoding) similarly fails as utility correlates with quality across instances but not within them.
6	Signal rankings depend on task difficulty, with consistent ordering across two model families at 8B scale.
7	RE findings rest on SciERC alone (preliminary, single benchmark) and warrant multi-benchmark validation.
8	A direct free-form generation ablation would confirm whether the bottleneck is decoding-regime-specific.
9	Trained verifiers, relaxed decoding constraints, scaling to 70B+ models, and conformal prediction are promising directions for closing the gap.
10	
```

</details>

1	# Free-form vs Constrained Decoding Ablation (Qwen SciERC N=8)
2	
3	**Date**: 2026-05-15
4	**Constrained baseline**: exp_012_rerun_1024 (551 instances × 8 samples)
5	**Free-form**: exp_freeform_ablation (551 instances × 8 samples, 4408 total)
6	
7	---
8	
9	## 1. Logprob Distribution Comparison
10	
11	| Metric | Constrained | Free-form | Ratio (F/C) |

14	| Median LP std | 0.0136 | 0.0137 | 1.01x |
15	| Mean LP range | 0.0623 | 0.0599 | 0.96x |
16	| Median LP range | 0.0413 | 0.0418 | 1.01x |
17	| Tied fraction (ε=0.05) | 59.7% | 57.7% | -2.0pp |
18	| N instances | 551 | 551 | — |
19

21

23

27	| LP-selected F1 | 0.6241 | 0.6222 |
28	| Selection gap | +0.0059 | +0.0018 |
29

31

33	|--------|------------|-----------|

35	| LP-selected F1 | 0.2845 | 0.3050 |
36	| Selection gap | -0.0068 | +0.0169 |
37

39

41	
42	| Signal | C_ρ | C_AUROC | F_ρ | F_AUROC | Δρ |
43	|--------|-----|---------|-----|---------|-----|
44	| SJ | 0.3599 | 0.7180 | 0.3774 | 0.7463 | +0.0175 |
45	| FK | 0.2665 | 0.7478 | 0.3197 | 0.7621 | +0.0532 |
46	| LP | 0.2052 | 0.6548 | 0.2129 | 0.6984 | +0.0077 |
47	| EM | 0.2945 | 0.4998 | 0.3171 | 0.5190 | +0.0225 |
48	| VC | 0.3792 | 0.7721 | 0.4089 | 0.7958 | +0.0297 |
49	
50	### RE (n=397)
51	
52	| Signal | C_ρ | C_AUROC | F_ρ | F_AUROC | Δρ |
53	|--------|-----|---------|-----|---------|-----|
54	| SJ | 0.2503 | 0.6099 | 0.2728 | 0.6173 | +0.0225 |
55	| FK | 0.2752 | 0.6638 | 0.3145 | 0.6837 | +0.0393 |
56	| LP | 0.2662 | 0.6780 | 0.3042 | 0.7025 | +0.0379 |
57	| EM | 0.1344 | 0.4886 | 0.1367 | 0.4877 | +0.0024 |

60	## 4. Parse Rate Statistics
61	
62	| Metric | Value |
63	|--------|-------|
64	| Total samples | 4408 |
65	| Successfully parsed | 4406 |
66	| Parse rate | 99.95% |
67	| 8/8 valid instances | 549 (99.6%) |
68	| 7/8 valid instances | 2 (0.4%) |
69	| Total failed samples | 2 |
70	
71	## 5. Key Findings
72	
73	1. **Score compression hypothesis NOT confirmed**: Free-form LP variance is virtually identical to constrained (ratio 0.97x for std, 0.96x for range). Removing constrained decoding did NOT meaningfully increase logprob spread. The tied fraction decreased by only 2.0pp (59.7% → 57.7%). This suggests logprob compression is intrinsic to the model/task, not an artifact of constrained decoding.
74	
75	2. **QE signals uniformly improve under free-form** (except RE voting_conf): All 5 signals show higher ρ in NER (+0.0077 to +0.0532). In RE, 4 of 5 signals improve (+0.0024 to +0.0393), with only VC declining (-0.0263). FK shows the largest consistent gains (NER +0.0532, RE +0.0393).
76	
77	3. **LP selection shows divergent behavior by subtask**: NER selection gain shrinks from +0.0059 (constrained) to +0.0018 (free-form). RE selection flips from negative (-0.0068) to strongly positive (+0.0169). Free-form enables effective LP-based selection for RE where constrained decoding failed.
78	
79	4. **Near-perfect parse rate (99.95%)**: Only 2 of 4408 samples failed parsing, and 549/551 instances (99.6%) have all 8 samples valid. Free-form decoding with Qwen on SciERC is highly reliable — constrained decoding is not needed for format compliance.
80	
81	5. **Greedy F1 is comparable across conditions**: NER greedy F1 differs by only 0.0022 (0.6182 vs 0.6204) and RE by 0.0033 (0.2913 vs 0.2880), confirming the comparison is apples-to-apples on a well-matched task difficulty.
82

291	    \ entity type 小幅拼写偏差 (~3%)，\n(c) free-form 下偶尔在 JSON 前后插入解释文本 (~3%)。\n90% 保证 n=476\
292	    \ NER (满足 ≥450 阈值)，统计功效充足。\n\n**Logprob variance 10x 恢复 (median range 0.031→0.32)**:\n\
293	    Constrained decoding 下 Outlines grammar mask 在每个 decode step 将 invalid tokens\
294	    \ 的\nlogit 设为 −∞，仅保留 grammar-valid tokens。对 JSON-schema IE：\n- 结构 tokens (\"{\"\
295	    、\"entities\"、\":\" 等) 在大多数 step 是唯一 valid token → logprob≈0\n- 仅 entity name/type\
296	    \ tokens 有选择自由 → 极少数 steps 贡献 variance\n- 8 samples 的 sequence logprob 被压缩到 ≤0.05\
297	    \ range (60% instances)\n\nFree-form 移除 mask 后：\n- 每步全 vocab (150k+ tokens) 可选\
298	    \ → token-level entropy 更高\n- 模型在 \"是否输出 entity\"、\"用什么格式\"、\"先输出哪个 entity\" 等决策点\n\

10	# 合并审稿报告 — Round 6
11	
12	**论文**: Strong Predictors, Weak Selectors: Confidence Signals for Test-Time Scaling in Information Extraction under Constrained Decoding
13	**目标 Venue**: EMNLP 2026 Findings
14	**Reviewer 1 侧重**: 方法严谨性 + 实验设计 | **Reviewer 2 侧重**: 新颖性 + 完整性 + 相关工作
15	**Reviewer 1 评分**: 3.0/5 | **Reviewer 2 评分**: 3.0/5
16	
17	---
18	
19	## Summary
20	
21	论文在 constrained decoding 下系统比较了 5 种 confidence signals（VC, SJ, FK, EM, logprob）用于 structured IE（NER + RE）的 quality estimation 和 sample selection。核心发现是 correlation-selection gap：信号是有效的 quality estimator（ρ=0.38, AUROC=0.75），但无法做 best-of-N selection（p>0.39）。机制诊断追溯到 constrained decoding 下的 logprob score compression。
22	
23	---
24	
25	## Strengths
26	
27	1. **统计分析严谨度远超同类论文**：Bootstrap CI、TOST 等价检验、Cohen's d、multi-seed stability analysis、diversity stratification、conditional filtering——两位 Reviewer 独立给出 4/5 Reproducibility，一致认为统计实践为 IE 领域少见的高标准。[AGREE R1+R2]
28	
29	2. **Logprob 压缩诊断是论文最有价值的贡献**：Non-tied subset analysis（+2.9pp, p=0.01, within-instance ρ: 0.08→0.39）不仅报告了 selection failure，还提供了 actionable 的机制解释——constrained decoding 压缩 logprob resolution 是主要瓶颈。两位 Reviewer 一致认为这是论文的核心洞察。[AGREE R1+R2]
30	
31	3. **Quality estimation vs. sample selection 的概念区分**：此前 IE 文献经常混淆这两个目标。论文清晰分离了 instance-level quality prediction 和 per-sample candidate ranking，为后续研究提供了有价值的框架。[AGREE R1+R2]
32	
33	4. **Difficulty gradient 设计合理**：三个 NER 数据集跨越 easy (CoNLL, 91% F1) → hard (SciERC) → boundary (WNUT-17, 52% zero F1)。WNUT-17 的 logprob 负相关（ρ=−0.410）和 conditional collapse（ρ=0.038, n.s.）是非显然发现。[AGREE R1+R2]
34	
35	5. **Limitations 的诚实程度值得肯定**：覆盖 RE 单数据集、模型规模限制、N-scaling 范围、thinking mode confound、logprob temperature gap。[AGREE R1+R2]
36	
37	---
38	
39	## Weaknesses（按严重度排序）
40	
41	### Must-Fix（影响接收决定）
42	
43	**[MF-1] Correlation-selection gap 的叙事结构需根本性重构** [共识 R1+R2]
44	- **问题**：论文将 "4/5 signals fail at selection" 与 logprob failure 并列呈现为同一现象（gap）。但 4/5 信号的 selection failure 是 **定义的直接推论**（instance-level 信号给同一 input 的所有 N 个 sample 相同分数），不需要实验验证。Discussion Section 5 自己承认了 "Layer 1 (definitional)"，但 Abstract 和 Introduction 仍将其包装为实证发现。
45	- **真正的实证贡献**是两个点：(a) consensus-proximity / MBR selection 也失败（Table 2: SJ selection 0.645 vs greedy 0.644）；(b) logprob（唯一 per-sample signal）因 score compression 失败。当前 framing 用 "5 signals all fail" 的统一叙事掩盖了这两个更有深度的发现。
46	- **修改要求**：Abstract 和 Introduction 重构——先一句话 dismiss instance-level 信号的 selection potential（"by definition, not empirically"），然后聚焦 consensus-proximity/MBR failure 和 logprob compression 作为核心贡献。Contribution 2 应以 logprob compression + MBR failure 为重心。
47	
48	**[MF-2] Contribution 1 framing 过度——benchmark 应用研究被呈现为 primary contribution** [共识 R1+R2]
49	- **问题**：将 "systematic multi-family comparison of 5 signals" 列为第一条 Contribution，给读者印象是论文的主要贡献。但本质是将已知 confidence signals（logprob, exact match, Fleiss' κ 等）应用于新任务设置——engineering benchmark 而非 new principle discovery。SJ 的 type-aware bipartite matching 有方法新意，但应被呈现为 methodology 而非独立 contribution。
50	- **修改要求**：Contribution 1 降级为 setup/methodology；Contribution 2（gap + logprob 压缩诊断）提升为核心。去掉 "first" claim（通过堆叠修饰词确保 "first" 成立属于 scope engineering）。
51	
52	**[MF-3] TOST δ 选择的理据概念错配** [共识 R1+R2，R1 列 must-fix，R2 agree]
53	- **问题**：δ 取值 ±2/3/5pp 的唯一理由是 IAA 变异（"well-annotated NER benchmarks typically achieve inter-annotator F1 above 95%"），但 IAA 测量标注噪声天花板，与 "selector 预期增益" 是不同概念。Oracle headroom = 13-18pp，δ=5pp 意味着放弃 ~38% 的可能增益仍算"等价"。
54	- **修改要求**：(a) 用 SESOI 框架或 oracle headroom 比例来论证 δ；(b) 报告 breakpoint δ*（使结论翻转的最小 δ）；(c) 更突出 effect size (Cohen's d=0.038) 和 bootstrap CI [−3.0, +4.2]pp 作为主要证据，TOST 作为补充。
55	
56	### Should-Fix（影响评分但不一票否决）
57	
58	**[SF-1] Constrained decoding scope 限定仍不充分** [共识方向一致，严重度分歧]
59	- Title 已含 "under Constrained Decoding"，但 Introduction 和 Discussion 中部分措辞仍暗示 gap 是 structured IE 的固有属性。Non-tied subset +2.9pp (p=0.01) 表明 gap 主要源于 score compression 而非 IE 固有属性。
60	- R1 要求 free-form 对照实验（listed critical）；R2 rebut：Layer 1 definitional gap 与 decoding 策略无关，free-form IE 实用性存疑。共识：不要求 free-form 实验，但 Introduction 和 Discussion 需各加 1 句明确声明 gap 发现 specific to constrained decoding regime。
61	
62	**[SF-2] Qwen3 thinking token 训练不一致** [共识 R1+R2]
63	- SciERC 未禁用 enable_thinking，CoNLL/WNUT 禁用。Thinking tokens 训练时影响 learned representations 可能改变 logprob 分布，进而影响 score compression 诊断（"60% instances tied" 可能在 thinking-disabled 下不同）。当前仅在 Limitations 脚注中提及。
64	- 修改要求：Experiments Setup §4.1 中显著声明此 confound。

_source line 73 · 2026-05-15T10:10:16.041Z_



Based on my thorough examination of the actual paper manuscript (`docs/paper/`), the project's experimental artifacts, the dream reports (which contain imagined/projected results), the actual free-form ablation results (`exp_freeform_ablation`), and six rounds of prior review history, here is my R1 review.

## R1 Review: Methods and Experiments

### Summary (2-3 sentences)

This paper systematically evaluates five confidence signals for best-of-N selection in LLM-based information extraction under constrained decoding, finding a "correlation-selection gap": signals that predict instance-level quality (rho up to 0.38, AUROC up to 0.75) fail to select better candidates despite 13-18pp oracle headroom. The mechanistic diagnosis traces this to log-probability score compression under grammar-guided decoding, with 60% of instances effectively tied. The experimental design is statistically rigorous---bootstrap CIs, permutation tests, TOST equivalence, multi-seed stability, diversity stratification---but the paper's central causal claim about constrained decoding as the bottleneck rests on indirect evidence (non-tied subset analysis) rather than the controlled ablation it proposes but does not execute.

### Strengths

- **S1: Statistical rigor far exceeds the IE literature norm.** The paper employs bootstrap resampling (B=10,000), permutation p-values, Cohen's d effect sizes, TOST equivalence testing, multi-seed stability analysis (3 seeds, sigma <= 0.025), diversity-stratified analysis, and pairwise bootstrap CIs for signal comparisons. This is among the most thorough statistical treatment I have seen in an empirical IE paper. The null result (NER LP +0.6pp, p=0.39, d=0.038) is reported honestly rather than spun.

- **S2: The per-sample vs. instance-level signal distinction is conceptually important and correctly handled.** The paper clearly formalizes that 4/5 signals assign a single score per input, making candidate ranking impossible by construction. The consensus-proximity strategy for instance-level signals (mathematically equivalent to leave-one-out SJ, as demonstrated in Appendix) is a principled MBR instantiation, not ad hoc. The paper correctly identifies that the interesting empirical questions are (a) whether MBR/consensus-proximity recovers discrimination and (b) whether log-probability exploits the headroom.

- **S3: The log-probability compression diagnosis is the paper's strongest contribution.** The non-tied subset analysis (instances with LP range >= 0.05, n=210) showing +2.9pp (p=0.01) and within-instance rho jumping from 0.08 to 0.39 provides compelling evidence that LP carries useful quality information that is resolution-limited under constrained decoding. This is not merely descriptive but mechanistically informative: the bottleneck is identified at the score-resolution level.

- **S4: The difficulty gradient is well-designed and yields non-obvious findings.** CoNLL (easy, 91% F1) through SciERC (hard) to WNUT-17 (boundary, 52% zero F1) reveals signal ranking crossovers (EM > SJ on easy tasks, SJ > EM on hard tasks) and the striking WNUT-17 LP negative correlation (rho = -0.410) with conditional collapse to rho = 0.038 (n.s.). The conditional filtering analysis throughout is rigorous and reveals that full-set correlations on high-failure-rate datasets conflate binary success prediction with quality gradation.

- **S5: Reproducibility details are comprehensive.** Training configuration table, prompt templates, LoRA hyperparameters, constrained decoding implementation (vLLM + XGrammar), evaluation protocol (type-grouped Hungarian matching), and the max(|g_i|, |g_j|) normalizer choice are all documented. The honest disclosure of the Qwen3 thinking-token training confound, while a weakness, demonstrates methodological transparency.

### Weaknesses

- **W1: The central causal claim is under-supported by the evidence presented.** The paper's framing---title "The Constrained Decoding Bottleneck," contribution C1, and Discussion---claims to have identified constrained decoding as the *cause* of selection failure. But the actual evidence is correlational: (a) LP scores are compressed under constrained decoding; (b) in the subset where compression is mitigated, selection improves. This is necessary but not sufficient for causal attribution. The paper itself acknowledges this: "A controlled ablation...would directly test whether the bottleneck is decoding-regime-specific" (Discussion, line 25-26) and marks the free-form experiment as "[Planned experiment]." Without this ablation, the claim should be substantially weakened to "LP compression *correlates with* selection failure" rather than "constrained decoding *causes* the bottleneck." The non-tied subset analysis has a selection bias confound: instances with wider LP range may be systematically different (e.g., more diverse outputs, different difficulty distribution) from tied instances, and the +2.9pp gain may reflect instance-level confounders rather than LP's discriminative power per se.

- **W2: The 2x2 model-dataset grid is thin for the generalization claims made.** Two 8B-parameter models from different families is a reasonable starting point, but the paper's language ("across model families," "model-agnostic") overstates what two data points establish. More critically, both models are fine-tuned with identical hyperparameters on the same data, so the "cross-model" comparison primarily tests architectural differences at a single scale and training recipe. Whether the findings hold for (a) larger models where output diversity and calibration differ substantially, (b) instruction-tuned models without task-specific fine-tuning, or (c) closed-source APIs where LP is unavailable remains entirely open. The CoNLL cross-model delta of 0.005 (SJ rho) is impressive but may reflect CoNLL's simplicity (88% identical outputs for LLaMA) rather than true model-invariance of the signals.

- **W3: N=8 as the primary evaluation point may be insufficient, and N-scaling analysis is limited.** The paper argues that "N=8 captures most quality estimation benefit" based on N=8 vs. N=16 comparisons showing sublinear gains. But the selection problem---the paper's core concern---is fundamentally different from quality estimation. With only 8 candidates and 60% of instances having LP range < 0.05, the candidate pool may simply be too small for meaningful selection. The oracle headroom at N=16 (Table in Appendix: LLaMA SciERC 0.806 vs. 0.778 at N=8) increases substantially, suggesting that larger N could change the selection landscape. The paper does not evaluate selection performance at N=16, only QE metrics, which is a gap given that the paper's central finding is about selection failure.

- **W4: The TOST equivalence testing delta justification is conceptually flawed.** The breakpoint delta* values (1.3pp to 6.5pp, representing 10-36% of oracle headroom) are useful, but the original motivation via inter-annotator agreement is a category error: annotation noise establishes a ceiling on measurable F1, not a threshold for practically meaningful selection gains. The revised anchoring to oracle headroom percentages (reported in Appendix) is better but still somewhat arbitrary. More importantly, TOST is a secondary analysis---the bootstrap CIs [-3.0, +4.2]pp spanning zero and Cohen's d=0.038 already establish the null result conclusively. The TOST section consumes space that could be better used for the missing free-form ablation.

- **W5: RE evidence is weak and inconsistently flagged.** A single RE benchmark (SciERC, 397 instances, 40% zero F1) with extreme per-relation variability (Feature-Of rho=0.004 vs. Hyponym-Of rho=0.646) does not support the RE-related claims scattered throughout the paper. The "preliminary" caveat appears in some table captions and the conclusion but is absent from the Abstract, Introduction contributions, and several Discussion paragraphs. Given that RE selection failure could stem from the 40% zero-F1 floor effect, entity span errors (55.5% of RE false negatives), or the compound error structure rather than the confidence signals per se, RE findings should be more consistently qualified or consolidated into a dedicated appendix section.

- **W6: The Qwen3 thinking-token training confound is inadequately addressed.** The SciERC checkpoint was trained with `enable_thinking=True`, injecting empty `&lt;think>&lt;/think>` blocks into training targets. While the paper argues the impact is minor ("<0.3% of training window"), this affects the loss computation for every training example and could alter the learned distribution in ways that specifically impact log-probability distributions. Since the LP compression diagnosis is the paper's core finding, and this confound affects precisely SciERC (the primary evaluation dataset), it deserves more than a footnote and limitations mention. At minimum, the paper should report whether the CoNLL checkpoint (thinking disabled) shows similar LP compression patterns---which it does (57-88% identical outputs, but this conflates ceiling effects with compression).

- **W7: No supervised upper bound despite 13-18pp oracle headroom.** The paper identifies a large gap between signal-based selection and the oracle but provides no estimate of how much a trained selector could recover. A simple logistic regression on the five signals (mentioned in the paper_framing as exp-016) was apparently run but showed ensemble underperformance vs. the best single signal. This negative result should be reported in the paper as it is informative about signal redundancy. More importantly, the absence of any per-sample learned selector (even a simple one trained on the available LP + output features) leaves the reader unable to assess whether the gap is fundamentally about score compression or about the signals themselves being informationally insufficient.

### Questions for Authors

- **Q1:** The non-tied subset (LP range >= 0.05, n=210) shows +2.9pp selection gain. What is the distribution of task difficulty (greedy F1, entity count, output diversity) in this subset compared to the tied subset? If non-tied instances are systematically easier or more diverse, the +2.9pp may not be attributable to LP discrimination alone.

- **Q2:** You report that the ensemble of all 5 signals (logistic regression, 5-fold CV) underperforms the best single signal (EM) by 8.3pp on NER AUROC. This is a striking negative result. Can you report this in the paper and discuss why? Feature correlation analysis (e.g., condition number of the signal matrix) would clarify whether this reflects collinearity.

- **Q3:** The consensus-proximity selection for SJ is mathematically equivalent to MBR with SJ as utility. Have you evaluated MBR with other utility functions---for instance, entity-level F1 approximation or BERTScore between linearized outputs---that might break the within-instance utility-quality misalignment?

- **Q4:** For the free-form ablation marked as "planned": given that the non-tied subset analysis is the evidentiary cornerstone of the causal claim, what is the timeline for this experiment? If it cannot be completed before submission, the title and abstract should be revised to remove "bottleneck" language.

- **Q5:** The FK signal shows negative excess agreement (-0.4% to -4.0%) across all configurations, and you argue this confirms FK is not inflated by inter-sample dependence. But negative excess agreement means samples are *anti-correlated*---erring on different items. Does this systematic anti-correlation pattern have implications for the diversity assumptions underlying the other consistency signals?

- **Q6:** At N=16, the oracle headroom on LLaMA SciERC NER increases from 13.2pp to 16.1pp (0.778 to 0.806). Do you have selection F1 results at N=16 for any signal? The paper only reports QE metrics at N=16, which is a significant gap given the paper's focus on selection.

### Minor Issues

- The paper title in `main.tex` ("Strong Predictors, Weak Selectors") differs from the title in the abstract description ("The Constrained Decoding Bottleneck"). Ensure consistency before submission.
- Table 1 caption states "N=16 results appear in Table X (Appendix)" but the appendix table only reports QE rho, not AUROC or selection F1 at N=16.
- The LOO-SJ equivalence proof in Appendix is correct but the "max per-instance score difference of 3.1e-4" is a numerical artifact of floating-point arithmetic, not a meaningful quantity. Remove or reframe.
- Equation 4 (SJ) uses M for the matched set and m for elements of M, but the text also uses M for the matching itself. Minor notation overloading.
- The WNUT-17 analysis uses n=689 after filtering gold-empty instances from 1,287. The filtering criterion and its justification should be stated explicitly.
- FK's adaptation to entity sets (treating unique (text, type) tuples as items with binary present/absent labels) is non-standard and deserves a brief justification for why this is preferable to, e.g., treating entity spans as the rating unit.
- The length vs. log-probability analysis (Appendix) reports *positive* correlation (rho=0.386), ruling out length-penalty confounding. But this raises a different concern: if longer outputs get higher LP, and longer outputs tend to have more entities, LP may be partially a proxy for output length rather than quality.

### Preliminary Scores

- **Soundness: 3.5/5** -- The experimental design is rigorous and the statistical methodology is exemplary, but the causal claim (constrained decoding as bottleneck) outstrips the evidence. The non-tied subset analysis is suggestive but has confounds; the planned free-form ablation is needed to support the paper's central thesis. RE evidence is thin.
- **Excitement: 2.5/5** -- The correlation-selection gap is a useful empirical finding, but much of it is definitional (4/5 signals cannot select by construction). The LP compression diagnosis is genuinely interesting but without the controlled ablation, the contribution is primarily diagnostic rather than mechanistic. The paper does not propose a solution.
- **Reproducibility: 4/5** -- Training configs, hyperparameters, evaluation protocols, and statistical procedures are well-documented. The Qwen3 thinking-token confound and the VC formula/implementation discrepancy are the main concerns.
- **Overall: 3.0/5** -- A methodologically careful negative-result paper with a genuinely interesting diagnostic finding (LP compression), but the causal framing exceeds the evidence, RE is under-powered, the model diversity is limited, and the absence of the free-form ablation or any supervised selector leaves the two most important follow-up questions unanswered. Suitable for Findings with revisions addressing the causal claim scope and RE qualification.
- **Confidence: 4/5** -- I have carefully examined the experimental design, statistical methodology, and all reported results. My main uncertainty is whether the free-form ablation (if completed) would substantially change the paper's contribution level.
